  `Claim Access`. A separate non-owner membership remains the required proof
  for a real grant/revoke lifecycle.

## D-2026-07-26-06 Separate Payment Identity From HONE Authentication

- Status: Accepted; implementation and local automated checks pass. Browser
  acceptance and real email-provider / non-owner purchase acceptance remain
  required before production release.
- Superseded by: `D-2026-08-03-01` for application-entitlement persistence,
  access-state policy, public API shape, and the international activation route.
  `D-2026-08-04-01` additionally supersedes every remaining Whop runtime,
  purchase, webhook, and Discord-fulfillment boundary. The separation between
  payment identity and HONE authentication remains in force.
- Context: International Whop checkout does not need to force a customer
  through Whop OAuth in order to use HONE, while mainland-China acquisition
  still needs a traceable phone identity. Payment, login, Discord linking, and
  application authorization are different concerns and must not be collapsed
  into one account-provider dependency.
- Decision: Existing domestic users keep the admin-admitted phone plus SMS
  path. An international Whop membership creates or updates a HONE external
  identity keyed by normalized purchase email, and the customer proves control
  of that email with a HONE-owned one-time-code flow. HONE does not use Whop
  OAuth as its application login and does not require a phone on this
  international path.
- Entitlement boundary: Only a signature-verified Standard Webhooks v1 event
  for the exact canonical company, product, and plan may change Whop
  application entitlement. The body is verified before JSON parsing, the
  delivery timestamp has a five-minute replay window, event IDs are
  idempotent, and older membership events cannot overwrite newer state.
  Checkout return parameters, email possession by itself, frontend state, and
  Discord roles never grant HONE product access.
- Access states: `active`, `trialing`, `past_due`, and the local
  cancel-at-period-end projection `canceling` continue to grant current-period
  access. Other Whop statuses retain account/session visibility through
  `/me` but receive HTTP `402` from paid product APIs. Existing domestic
  invite users remain independent of Whop because HONE does not yet own a
  canonical Knowledge Planet entitlement feed.
- Persistence: Local mode stores external identity, hashed email challenge,
  and the latest membership projection beside `web_invite_users` in SQLite.
  Cloud mode embeds the same backward-compatible external state in the
  existing PG web-user record until a measured query/race requirement justifies
  a dedicated indexed table. Neither path stores a plaintext email code or
  full payment-card data.
- Delivery and Discord: Email delivery is an injected
  `EmailVerificationSender`; `D-2026-07-28-01` selects Cloudflare Email
  Sending while preserving the deliberately unconfigured fail-closed default.
  Whop's native Discord app remains the sole
  owner of Discord account linking and `VIP 付费用户` role grant/removal under
  D-2026-07-26-04; HONE's webhook owns application access only.
- Follow-up: Before production, configure a transactional email provider and
  the Whop webhook secret, add refund/dispute handling plus periodic
  reconciliation, and run a non-owner buy → email activate → HONE access →
  Discord claim → cancel/revoke → repurchase acceptance matrix.

## D-2026-08-03-01 Make Billing A Provider-Neutral Domain Without A Compatibility Layer

- Status: Superseded in its multi-provider and Whop-runtime scope by
  `D-2026-08-04-01`. Its Stripe event-ordering, durable-inbox, identity
  separation, server-authoritative access, and no-compatibility decisions remain.
- Created: 2026-08-03
- Owner: Codex
- Supersedes: `D-2026-07-26-06` only for application-entitlement persistence,
  access policy, provider-specific public fields, and `/activate/whop`.
- Related files: `memory/src/billing.rs`, `memory/src/web_auth.rs`,
  `crates/hone-web-api/src/routes/{billing,stripe,whop}.rs`,
  `packages/app/src/pages/{public-activate,public-me,public-plan}.tsx`
- Related docs: `docs/runbooks/stripe-billing.md`,
  `docs/runbooks/whop-hone-activation.md`
- Context: Adding Stripe beside Whop would otherwise spread provider-specific
  identity fields and access checks through authentication, public APIs, and
  UI. Dual-reading the old Whop projection while adding a second ledger would
  create two access truths, make out-of-order revocation unsafe, and preserve
  an architecture the owner explicitly authorized replacing.
- Decision: Identity, Billing persistence, access policy, and provider adapters
  are separate layers. SQLite and PostgreSQL persist canonical
  `billing_entitlements` plus `billing_webhook_events`. Stripe and Whop verify
  raw signed events, enforce exact catalog boundaries, normalize only fields
  needed for access, and write the same ledger. Paid access is granted when any
  row is active or in an unexpired bounded grace period. A redirect, email
  login, frontend state, or Discord role never grants access.
- Migration: Startup performs a one-time forward migration of legacy Whop JSON
  into the Billing ledger, then removes old SQLite columns and rewrites cloud
  identity records in canonical form. Runtime APIs expose `identity_kind` plus
  provider-neutral `billing`; there is no dual-read, old `whop_membership`
  response, or `/activate/whop` route.
- Stripe boundary: Checkout and Portal are authenticated same-origin server
  operations. The browser cannot submit price/product IDs. Test/live key modes
  cannot mix. Checkout can be disabled independently while webhooks and Portal
  remain available. First access requires a paid event; subscription status by
  itself cannot activate a never-paid account. HONE iOS exposes only login,
  restore, and status; separate server-owned purchase and management policies
  hide external digital-purchase pricing, calls to action, Portal, and Whop
  management links fail-closed. The unified activation page also resolves its
  adapter against that server policy: explicit Whop remains available for
  recovery, while an explicit Stripe URL falls back to Whop whenever Checkout
  is disabled instead of presenting a form whose server action must fail.
- Stripe ordering and endpoint boundary: `checkout.session.completed` is only a
  provisional pending marker and orders from the Checkout Session creation
  time, because Stripe can create the authoritative invoice/subscription
  events immediately before the later completion envelope. The inbox retains
  the actual envelope time for audit. Local CLI, registered test, and
  registered live destinations have distinct signing secrets; a deployment
  accepts exactly one `HONE_STRIPE_MODE` and endpoint secret, and
  `HONE_STRIPE_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` is never treated as webhook configuration.
- Consequences: This is a deliberate breaking internal/public API migration.
  All in-repository callers move in the same change. Rollback disables new
  Checkout but does not delete ledger rows or stop webhook ingestion. Missed
  event reconciliation and owner-approved refund/dispute automation remain
  explicit follow-ups.
- Recovery: Webhook projection runs from the durable inbox with bounded retry,
  a five-minute lease, and a periodic recovery scan. Completion is conditional
  on the claim's attempt count, preventing an expired worker from overwriting a
  newer claim; provider timestamps separately protect entitlement state from
  out-of-order events.
- Verification: Provider-neutral storage tests cover multi-provider access,
  stale-event rejection, webhook idempotency/hash conflicts, processing state,
  finite grace, and one-time legacy projection removal. Stripe tests cover raw
  signature verification, current Invoice/Subscription shapes, exact catalog
  filtering, paid-versus-status signals, and the real provider topology where
  invoice/subscription event timestamps precede Checkout completion. Frontend contracts cover the
  single activation route, webhook-authoritative success polling, duplicate
  warnings, Portal, and iOS purchase/management hiding. A CI-safe regression
  starts an isolated real backend and drives raw signed Stripe/Whop HTTP events
  through the durable inbox without external credentials. A separate opt-in
  external regression uses a disposable Stripe catalog, Test Clock, customer,
  subscriptions, invoices, Checkout Session, and Portal Session to prove the
  real paid/failure/recovery/cancel/repurchase lifecycle before deleting the
  clock-owned objects and archiving the catalog. The later owner decision and
  production cutover are recorded in
  `docs/archive/plans/stripe-whop-parallel-billing.md`.

## D-2026-08-04-01 Make Stripe The Only External Billing Provider

- Status: Accepted and production-complete on exact commit
  `edddfc5b890d124d76d8c6eddc9aa85f2e94b807`.
- Created: 2026-08-04
- Owner: Codex
- Supersedes: `D-2026-07-26-04`, all Whop-specific portions of
  `D-2026-07-26-06`, and the multi-provider/runtime compatibility portions of
  `D-2026-08-03-01`.
- Related files: `memory/src/{billing,web_auth}.rs`,
  `crates/hone-core/src/cloud_runtime.rs`,
  `crates/hone-web-api/src/routes/{billing,stripe}.rs`,
  `packages/app/src/pages/{public-activate,public-me,public-plan}.tsx`
- Related docs: `docs/archive/plans/stripe-whop-parallel-billing.md`,
  `docs/runbooks/stripe-billing.md`
- Context: The owner explicitly directed HONE to stop validating or preserving
  the retired payment channel, archive its external resources, move all future
  subscriptions to Stripe, and remove compatibility code. Production inventory
  found zero retired-provider entitlement rows, zero retired-provider webhook
  rows, and zero cloud user records containing its legacy projection, so there
  is no paid-member dataset to migrate.
- Decision: Stripe is HONE's only external subscription authority. The HONE
  Billing ledger remains the application-authorization truth source and keeps
  domestic invite authority separate, but external entitlement/webhook rows are
  constrained to Stripe. The public application exposes one `/activate` route,
  one Stripe Checkout adapter, one Stripe Portal adapter, and one verified
  Stripe webhook endpoint. It contains no provider selector, fallback purchase
  channel, legacy recovery route, runtime environment variables, adapter, or
  management link for the retired provider.
- Migration: SQLite performs a one-time table rebuild when old provider
  constraints are detected and copies only Stripe/domestic-invite entitlements
  plus Stripe webhook rows. PostgreSQL migration
  `20260804_stripe_only_billing` holds an advisory transaction lock, deletes
  non-target rows, replaces both provider constraints, and records completion.
  This is deliberately destructive and forward-only; there is no dual read,
  tombstone adapter, or compatibility window.
- Stripe authority: Live account `acct_1U0D6UEK7h1dD4JH` must remain fully
  enabled with no requirements. Production uses Product
  `prod_V0FIIUS22IGljn`, annual Price
  `price_1U0Eo6EK7h1dD4JHDrhlnPw8`, registered webhook destination
  `we_1U0c0XEK7h1dD4JHrvQ9CRaH`, and a permanent restricted live key with only
  Checkout Sessions (v1) and Customer Portal write permissions. Runtime key
  validation accepts same-mode standard (`sk_*`) and restricted (`rk_*`) keys
  while rejecting every test/live mismatch.
- Access and event boundary: Checkout redirects, email verification, frontend
  state, and query parameters do not grant paid access. Only signature-verified
  events for the exact mode/Product/Price mutate the durable inbox and ledger.
  Existing provisional ordering, idempotency, bounded retry, attempt fencing,
  grace-period, stale-event, and old-subscription protections remain unchanged.
- Rollout: Build and push one exact revision, install live credentials only via
  owner-only deployment storage, verify two idle active-chat readings, switch
  the immutable release, and accept database migration, public auth boundaries,
  Checkout creation, Portal creation, webhook rejection/acceptance, and
  production pages. Archive retired external products/plans/webhooks only after
  the Stripe public smoke passes.
- Rollback: Disable new Stripe Checkout or restore the previous immutable code
  release. Keep the live Stripe webhook and existing customer/subscription/
  invoice/ledger data active; never re-enable the retired provider as an
  application fallback.
- Verification: Stripe-only storage and HTTP lifecycle tests, negative runtime
  source scans, frontend contracts, Web tests, restricted-key mode tests, full
  repository gates, exact revision checks, redacted production screenshots,
  and post-deploy PostgreSQL constraints/data counts are required before this
  decision is marked production-complete. Those checks passed: production
  reports the exact source revision above from a verified GHCR bundle, uses
  Stripe live mode with the exact catalog and no retired-provider runtime
  variables, rejects invalid webhooks without mutation, creates an official
  live USD 199.99/year Checkout, and preserves inactive entitlement while that
  Session remains unpaid. The retired Whop product/plan are hidden and the HONE
  webhook is deleted.

## D-2026-08-04-03 Build Managed Linux Runtimes In Actions And Deliver Them Through GHCR

- Status: Accepted and production-verified on exact commit
  `edddfc5b890d124d76d8c6eddc9aa85f2e94b807` and manifest digest
  `sha256:0dcd14a825a124344908b34f6cab19f83eca1f614a40eb2bdf08df2f093f0eee`.
- Created: 2026-08-04
- Owner: Codex
- Related files: `.github/workflows/runtime-image.yml`,
  `deploy/runtime/Dockerfile`, `scripts/package_runtime_bundle.sh`,
  `scripts/verify_runtime_bundle.sh`, `scripts/stage_ghcr_runtime.sh`,
  `tests/regression/ci/test_runtime_image_contract.sh`
- Related docs: `docs/runbooks/backend-deployment.md`,
  `docs/archive/plans/stripe-whop-parallel-billing.md`
- Context: The workstation is macOS while production is Debian Linux, and
  compiling the large workspace on the production VM is slow, consumes several
  GiB of transient disk, and couples deployment safety to mutable build-host
  state. The owner requested a Linux-image build and GHCR delivery instead.
- Decision: GitHub Actions builds the six managed server binaries only inside
  digest-pinned Debian Bookworm `linux/amd64`. A pinned `cargo-chef` recipe separates
  dependency compilation from changing workspace source, and BuildKit exports
  that dependency layer into a named GHA cache scope. A final `scratch` image contains only the
  release bundle; Git SHA, UTC build time, source kind, Cargo profile, and target
  appear in strict metadata, while the same SHA is compiled into build info and
  attached as an OCI label. The image is linked to the source repository before
  first publication and is addressed in production by manifest digest, never by
  mutable `main`.
- Host boundary: Production retains systemd, its protected environment file,
  `/opt/hone/releases`, and atomic `/opt/hone/current`. It installs no Docker
  daemon. A checksum-pinned static `crane` exports the image; the staging script
  rejects symlinks, missing/extra files, malformed metadata, hash mismatches,
  or a revision mismatch, then stops without changing current or restarting a
  service. Existing two-read active-chat drain and rollback rules remain the
  only cutover authority.
- Secrets and visibility: Runtime images contain compiled repository
  source outputs, `soul.md`, the stock-research Skill, verification tooling, and
  metadata only. They contain no `.env`, config, credentials, data, logs, or
  local worktrees. Prefer anonymous pull when organization policy permits a
  public repository-linked package. When policy keeps the package private,
  production may use only a short-lived or operator-provided credential scoped
  to `read:packages`, supplied over standard input into a temporary `0700`
  `DOCKER_CONFIG` and deleted immediately after export. Never persist a broad
  personal token or registry login on the host.
- Rollback: Keep the previous release directory and environment backup. A bad
  image is rejected before cutover; a post-cutover failure restores the prior
  symlink and restarts through the same systemd boundary after a fresh drain.
  GHCR adoption does not change application/database rollback semantics.
- Verification: The CI-safe contract proves fixed Linux architecture, pinned
  Debian builder, scoped GHA cache, repository label, scratch final image,
  complete bundle verification, and tamper rejection without network or Docker.
  Runtime Image run `30893733765` passed in about 5 minutes 23 seconds. GCE
  exported the exact private digest daemonlessly with ephemeral minimal auth;
  staging verified metadata, embedded SHA and every payload hash before the
  atomic cutover. `/api/meta`, cloud authority, ports, auth boundaries, service
  stability and the retained rollback release all passed after cutover.

## D-2026-08-04-04 Make Discord Community Operations Stripe-only

- Status: Accepted and externally verified in guild
  `1391380994182877205` on 2026-08-04.
- Created: 2026-08-04
- Owner: Codex + owner
- Supersedes: the current-operation portions of `D-2026-07-26-04` and the
  Discord role-fulfillment portions of `D-2026-07-26-06`; their historical
  evidence remains valid for the retired Whop stage.
- Related docs: `docs/runbooks/discord-stripe-community.md`,
  `docs/archive/plans/discord-stripe-community-migration.md`,
  `docs/handoffs/2026-08-04-discord-stripe-community-migration.md`
- Context: Stripe-only Billing was live and Whop purchase/webhook resources were
  retired, but the public Discord membership pin still linked to Whop Checkout
  and promised automatic Whop role delivery. The backend log channel still used
  the provider name. HONE had a protected local bot token, but that bot was not
  a member of the production community.
- Decision: Install the dedicated HONE bot in the production guild, present it
  as `HONE 社区助手`, and make all current membership copy point only to HONE
  `/activate`, Stripe Checkout, and `/me`. Replace the public stale message only
  after the new pin is verified. Rename the old provider log channel as a
  restricted historical archive; retain its contents for now because they are
  operational records that may contain PII.
- Permission exception: The owner approved Discord Administrator after the
  narrower Manage Guild/Channels/Roles/Messages grant was proven unable to
  cross an existing channel-level send deny, and Discord rejected a managed bot
  role changing its own overwrite. The token therefore has production-admin
  impact and must remain in a mode-`0600`, Git-ignored config. The local listener
  stays disabled until a separate runtime behavior review.
- Authority boundary: Discord roles remain community presentation only. HONE
  Billing is the application-access truth source and Stripe is the sole external
  subscription authority. Until a separately designed reconciliation system
  exists, operations may grant `VIP 付费用户` only after protected HONE admin
  confirmation; email, screenshots, redirects, Discord state, and historical
  provider logs cannot grant HONE access.
- Verification: Discord API confirmed identity, guild membership, Administrator
  and management permissions, channels, roles, integrations, and zero guild
  webhooks. The replacement pin passed content/link readback; the old message
  deleted and returned `404`; the renamed historical channel and nickname passed
  exact readback. External Chrome showed the final Stripe-only message and no
  visible Whop text. No unrelated member, role, message, or historical log was
  changed.

## D-2026-07-26-05 Let Representative Broad-Market Quotes Satisfy A Narrow Evidence Floor

- Status: Accepted and production-verified on exact commit `84ca1f2114c059a157cd893c84067638c7618e84`.
- Refines: `D-2026-07-22-01`, `D-2026-07-26-01`, `D-2026-07-26-02`, `D-2026-07-26-03`, and `docs/adr/0004-agent-owned-research-loop.md`. Ordinary named-security identity routes, exact visible/persisted bytes, current-result inspection, and market-move pre-publication consistency checks remain unchanged.
- Incident evidence: Exact `e46a6bf439c1ab5c1e68a5788a9a0ad82f760a73` was pushed and deployed. Fresh actor `codex-canary-e46a6bf4-broad-20260726-1715` asked `美股为什么大跌`. The model first emitted SPY/QQQ/IWM quote calls with the exact codes left in `query`, so DataFetch returned `证券代码不能为空`; it then retried SPY/QQQ correctly with `symbol`, and both quotes succeeded. The ledger nevertheless treated those broad benchmarks as ordinary named securities requiring identity search, kept `ToolChoiceMode::Required`, and induced a later post-header quote batch that again used `query`. Because the post-commit structural validator and executor did not share a compatible target for that shape, the run ended with the explicit partial research-failure suffix instead of a market answer. This was a request-shape/evidence-floor deadlock, not missing quote data.
- Exact-symbol compatibility: The shared DataFetch target parser accepts `query` for a non-search symbol-scoped request only when that same call explicitly declares `identity_match=exact_symbol`. `ticker` and then `symbol` retain precedence; a missing/non-exact match mode, non-string query, or malformed higher-precedence field fails closed. Executor routing, finance activation, ledger symbol observation, and post-commit registered/read-only structural validation all consume this one parser, so an exact-symbol compatibility call cannot execute under one target while telemetry or safety evaluates another. Natural company names never gain this alias.
- Broad-market floor: Only a request already classified by the server-generated market-move anchor as a broad US-market question enters `broad_market_mode`. Attempts to obtain full `quote` or `snapshot` evidence for at least two distinct representative groups—S&P 500, Nasdaq, Dow, or Russell 2000 through their bounded ETF/index symbols—satisfy the structural research floor without synthetic per-ETF identity searches. `quote_short` cannot open the floor because it may omit percentage, exchange, and provider-time fields. One benchmark, duplicate aliases within one group, unrelated securities, and every ordinary single-/multi-security turn remain below the existing identity-route floor. Attempts do not assert provider success: only actual verified full-quote results can open the final evidence boundary, and after two such groups plus one source-search attempt, the same Agent receives a bounded tools-disabled final instead of another equivalent search round. The Agent and deterministic fallback must still disclose failures and meet D-2026-07-26-03 date/scope/quote-field/cause checks.
- Guidance and observability: When no identity route exists in broad-market mode, the next-round guidance asks only for the missing representative group or, after two groups, instructs the same Agent to inspect quote results and obtain same-day causal sources as needed. It does not replay the ordinary “search every named security” instruction. Audit metadata records `broad_market_mode` and the distinct attempted quote groups so a live canary can prove why `Required → Auto` changed.
- Verification and rollout: The shared parser regression covers exact-symbol query acceptance, ticker/symbol precedence, missing/non-exact modes, wrong types, and security-target parity. Agent regressions prove one group and `quote_short` are insufficient, two full-quote groups open the floor, ordinary securities still require identity search, the query-alias call passes the registered read-only validator, and verified groups plus one Web attempt force one bounded final without duplicate research. Final Agent `134/134`, Hone Tools `143` with one optional dependency test ignored, finance CI `42/42`, the complete repository contract, exact immutable deployment, and the four production canaries pass. Web and Feishu run from the same exact build in isolated processes; the configured Discord credential is rejected by its gateway and remains quarantined so it cannot terminate the healthy roles.

## D-2026-07-26-06 Buffer Interactive Answers And Recover Without Generic Business Refusal

- Status: Accepted and production-verified on exact runtime commit `75ca1957ebceaa3f8d564d423bcb6f8940b5c74e`.
- Supersedes in part: `D-2026-07-21-01`, `D-2026-07-22-01`, and the prefix-failure portions of `D-2026-07-26-02`. The established finance first-line bytes, one-Agent/context model, bounded research, exact visible/history equality, current-turn evidence rules, mutation replay safety, and the market-move boundary in `D-2026-07-26-03` remain active. What is superseded is early Interactive prefix/body ACK and the fixed suffix `本轮研究未能完成，暂未形成可供参考的标的结论。`.
- Incident evidence: Web actor `Actor_web__direct__web-user-cb0c3ef59f8d` submitted three portfolio screenshots at 19:24, 19:26, and 19:27 CST on 2026-07-26. Each attachment downloaded successfully, but the model received only local paths and called non-executable `skill_tool(image_understanding)`. The finance effect validator classified that prompt-loading call as unknown/persistent, rejected the run after the neutral header had already crossed the Web boundary, and Session appended the fixed research-failure suffix. The same image failure class had recurred on 2026-06-06, 2026-07-14, 2026-07-15, 2026-07-20, and 2026-07-23. The active MiniMax request was text-only, so the skill's earlier claim that the underlying model could see the referenced image was also false.
- Attachment decision: On macOS, a successfully downloaded image is OCRed with local Apple Vision before model execution. Bounded normalized rows enter the trusted current-turn `【图片文字提取】` block grouped by filename. The image skill consumes that evidence and never claims that a path-only text request is multimodal. Partial/empty OCR narrows only the missing fields; it cannot justify a generic refusal or internal storage/toolchain diagnostics. Unsupported hosts do not fabricate text. A non-executable `skill_tool` call only loads prompt/metadata and is classified known read-only; `execute_script=true` remains write-capable/unknown and is never generalized as safe.
- Tool and model recovery decision: After finance activates, any batch containing an unregistered, malformed, structurally invalid, unknown-effect, or write-capable call is blocked in full before assistant framing, observer notification, registry execution, or network access. Blocking does not authorize refusal: the same Agent receives one tools-disabled continuation and must answer from already acquired evidence, with only a narrow missing-fact disclosure. A production active empty stream receives its existing bounded retry; one-off provider/protocol error or step timeout then receives the same no-tools continuation. A second failure remains an infrastructure failure. Persistent tool execution failures and uncertain mutations stop without replay and never claim success.
- Publication decision: Web finance still receives the exact Session-time prefix contract and the model must keep the existing answer format. Production sets `commit_before_model=false` and leaves `TerminalStreamPolicy` disabled, so the complete Interactive final crosses `DeferredUserOutputEmitter` once only after it is usable. A recoverable failure can no longer strand a neutral header, and an irrecoverable failure publishes neither a business prefix/body nor canned research-failure copy. This intentionally trades the earlier sub-second header ACK for answer integrity; it does not change the prompt's first-line or section contract.
- Domain decision: Hone remains finance-centered, but ordinary non-finance questions and greetings reach the same configured Agent and receive a direct concise answer. Finance tools, time-first formatting, and stale finance context are not injected into such answers. The previous hard-coded non-finance boundary reply and prompt-level blanket refusal are removed.
- Verification and rollout: Agent `135/135` and Channels `680/680` with one host-dependent OCR test ignored pass. Focused tests prove real Apple Vision extraction from the incident attachment, trusted OCR prompt construction, unchanged finance prefix bytes under whole-answer buffering, ordinary non-finance Agent execution, non-executable image skill admission, zero execution of unsafe/mixed batches, same-Agent answers after blocked tools/empty streams/provider errors/step timeouts, recovery before publication, and no canned partial after a double incomplete stream. Finance CI is `43/43`; Web is `294/294`, Edge Worker is `45/45`, and the complete workspace/CI-safe gates pass. Exact `75ca1957` was built into a 501-file verified package and deployed after zero-active-chat drain. Cloud authority, PostgreSQL, object storage, ports, auth boundaries, Web and Feishu are healthy. Fresh production market, non-finance, and real-OCR attachment-block replays each produced one successful terminal, two-row byte-identical visible/history content, no fixed refusal, and zero active chats.

## D-2026-07-26-07 Make Context-Window Recovery Invisible And Current-Turn Aware

- Status: Accepted and production-verified on exact commit `620391d1f137d433d89a34f944b5076463936d62`.
- Refines: D-2026-07-26-06 and `docs/adr/0004-agent-owned-research-loop.md`. The one-Agent/output boundary, unchanged finance first-line and answer structure, full-result auditability, current-turn evidence rules, exact visible/history bytes, and execute-once mutation safety remain active. It supersedes the hard-coded user-facing context-overflow terminal and the assumption that durable Session compaction alone can recover every model request.
- Incident evidence: Exact production runtime `75ca1957` received the 47-character Web question `最近AI概念股票疯狂回调，未来一个月结合宏观，及重要事件，重要财报等分析未来一个月可能的走势。` at `20:54:24` CST. The first attempt successfully executed nine quote/calendar/Web calls before its next model request overflowed. Forced compaction reduced 18 durable messages totaling about 50,069 bytes to a 1,777-character summary plus six retained items, but the new Agent attempt executed another nine read-only tools and overflowed again. At `105.982s` Session published and persisted the hard-coded context/`/compact`/new-session instruction. The decisive growth was the current-turn raw tool transcript plus tool schema/system contract; history-only compaction repeated cost without bounding it.
- Agent recovery: When a read-only FunctionCalling turn has real current-turn tool results and the next provider call reports a recognized context-window/request-too-large error, the same `Agent::run` performs no tool replay. It constructs a deterministic valid-JSON evidence copy containing every executed call's id, name, bounded arguments, and bounded result. Full results remain in `tool_calls_made`, audit, and the in-memory execution trace; omitted material is explicitly marked unavailable. Compaction uses a fixed number of linear passes: a far-over-budget value becomes one bounded serialized preview, and the request path never removes one array tail and reserializes the entire value repeatedly. The same Agent then receives `tools=[]`, the original current request, the bounded evidence copy, and the existing final-answer/evidence contract. This is a same-loop continuation, not `finish_research`, a handoff, a new semantic role, an answer rewrite, or a second visible response.
- Session recovery: Overflow before usable current-turn evidence keeps one forced boundary-based compact retry. If that retry also overflows, a final read-only attempt restores no historical messages, compact summary, assistant/tool protocol, or invoked-skill snapshots; it rebuilds only the live Session time/system contract and current runtime input. No persistent, uncertain, write-capable, send, schedule, delete, or trade operation may enter either automatic replay path.
- Visibility rule: Context-window, request-size, token-budget, compaction, `/compact`, absolute-path, and “open a new session” diagnostics are internal telemetry only. They may not enter channel errors, assistant rows, scheduler delivery previews, or user-facing fallback constants. Exhausting the bounded recovery remains a sanitized infrastructure failure and must not fabricate evidence or mutation success.
- Verification and rollout: Regressions must cover oversized current-turn tool payload recovery, deterministic evidence bounds and source identity, a large calendar result completing under a bounded performance ceiling with no iterative tail-pop helper, exact finance prefix preservation, no repeated tool execution, double-overflow current-turn-only restore, summary/skill/tool exclusion, persistent-operation no-retry, and a repository static ban on the incident copy. The first exact `268561c4` production replay triggered the intended overflow path but exposed the now-prohibited quadratic array-tail serializer: `hone-console-page` held one core at about 99% and starved health requests until rollback. Replacement production acceptance therefore requires an exact immutable package, zero-active-chat controlled restart, storage/auth/port health, and a deliberately long fresh Web actor replaying the incident wording with one successful terminal, unchanged finance format, no reset/error/internal-limit copy, byte-identical two-row history, responsive concurrent health probes, and active chats returning to zero.

## D-2026-07-27-01 Make Evidence Admission And Mutation Completion Capability-Level

- Status: Accepted and production-verified from fix commit `c2edceb7269476c39a3eb23efd25d14d4675aa93` in the current exact runtime `f56631072a38f32f8f02efa49c5a268156612219`.
- Context: The nineteen-row experience workbook mixes old incidents, current product defects, UI preferences, and one non-product asset request. Treating every row as an independent bug would reintroduce company aliases, phrase detectors, output regexes, CSS coordinates, and channel-only rewrites. Two recurrent trust failures remain systemic: symbol-specific earnings questions depend on whether an Agent happens to combine a global date-window calendar with other endpoints, and an execute-once mutation error is surfaced as an ambiguous user conclusion even when the actor's durable state is safely readable.
- Evidence decision: Add `earnings_outlook` as one symbol-scoped composite DataFetch contract. It independently gathers quote, company earnings timing/estimates, quarterly analyst estimates, price-target consensus, ratings snapshot, and financials; it keeps component errors and coverage instead of turning a partial provider failure into a whole-answer failure. Quote fields receive separate price/change/range/market-cap usability flags. Target consensus with an invalid internal range is quarantined; an extreme target/current-price ratio remains visible but requires independent corroboration. The raw provider payload remains available for audit. This refines upstream evidence admission only: no generic post-generation judge, second synthesizer, phrase/company exception, or answer-format rewrite is introduced.
- Mutation decision: Extend the canonical tool-effect classifier with explicit actor-scoped read-after-write contracts for `portfolio → view`, `cron_job → list`, and `notification_prefs → get_overview`. When one of those writes returns an ambiguous error, execute the write exactly once, perform only the safe read, and give the same Agent one tools-disabled completion based on observed state. Never replay the mutation and never surface `可能已执行` or `状态不确定` when authoritative state was observed. Tools without a safe state read remain fail-closed.
- Projection decision: Content semantics stay canonical while channels remain native projections. Feishu keeps native JSON 2.0 tables and Web keeps the shared Markdown renderer. The renderer treats accidental GFM deletion spans as inert text so paired tildes cannot strike an entire finance paragraph; this does not change the established prompt or answer section contract.
- Product triage boundary: A workbook row may be `no_action`, `verified_existing`, `resolved_systemically`, or `deferred_no_safe_general_solution`. Non-product assets and suggestions whose only implementation is a risky single-point patch are not mandatory code changes. The final workbook must preserve all source rows and give each one a status, capability domain, rationale, evidence, and production state, plus an overall summary.
- Verification and rollout: Focused tests cover field-level quote quarantine, target-price dimensional warnings, partial earnings coverage, route-floor admission, execute-once mutation plus read reconciliation, and safe Markdown rendering. Full Rust/Web/Edge/regression gates and the visually inspected replacement workbook passed on 2026-07-27. Commit `c2edceb7` was pushed and first assembled into a verified immutable package. Concurrent runtime changes required a second exact build; the resulting supervisor restart exposed that cloud-backed `/api/meta` plus proxy-eligible loopback requests could kill an otherwise healthy child after the readiness deadline. Commits `e07eadb3` and `f5663107` moved readiness to a lightweight local endpoint and force loopback supervisor clients to bypass proxies. Exact `f5663107` is now live as a verified `502`-payload package and remained healthy beyond the old self-exit window. Process paths and working directories, ports `8077/8088`, authoritative PostgreSQL/R2 storage, zero local durable dependencies, local/origin/public JSON `401` auth boundaries, public routes/security headers, live Feishu connectivity, and repeated zero active chats passed. A separate transient R2 failure reproduced with both old and new binaries and was traced to the host's failed Clash global node; selecting Clash `DIRECT` restored storage health immediately.

## D-2026-07-28-01 Use Cloudflare Email Sending For Purchase-Email Verification

- Status: Accepted and production-verified on exact runtime
  `482c34d54aef4f0d9726acea0b753d751a5973be`. On 2026-07-28 the owner approved
  Workers Paid at `$5/month + usage`; Billing shows the subscription active,
  `hone-claw.com` Email Sending is enabled with configured DNS, and an
  account-scoped `Email Sending: Edit` token is installed in the ignored local
  runtime environment. Two controlled sends are `Delivered`, and the owner
  confirmed real inbox receipt by returning a verification code without that
  value entering repository artifacts. Source is published directly through
  `main`; complete runtime configuration is loaded from the ignored owner-only
  environment and the controlled production restart has completed.
- Context: HONE already uses Cloudflare DNS and the public site, while the Whop
  international activation flow requires arbitrary-recipient transactional
  email. Cloudflare Email Service now exposes a native REST API, so adding a
  separate Resend/SendGrid account or moving the backend into a Worker is not
  required. The logged-in dashboard must still confirm that the existing
  account has Email Sending entitlement; any paid-plan upgrade remains an
  owner decision.
- Decision: The concrete `EmailVerificationSender` calls Cloudflare's
  structured REST endpoint from the Rust backend. It uses one
  account-scoped token with only `Email Sending: Edit`, an onboarded
  `hone-claw.com` sender address, and the existing bounded `reqwest` client.
  SMTP and a Worker forwarding hop are excluded because neither improves the
  trust boundary for this backend and both add another runtime dependency.
- Configuration boundary: `HONE_CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`,
  `HONE_CLOUDFLARE_EMAIL_API_TOKEN`, and `HONE_EMAIL_FROM` are runtime-only
  values. All three absent preserves the existing fail-closed `503`; a partial
  or structurally invalid configuration fails startup. No token, purchase
  email, plaintext code, provider body, or raw API response may enter
  committed files or logs.
- Delivery boundary: Each call has one recipient plus plain-text and HTML
  content. A `2xx` envelope is not sufficient by itself: Cloudflare must report
  the recipient as delivered or queued, or return a non-empty provider message
  ID; any permanent bounce is a failure. The message-ID path is required by the
  live Email Sending beta response, which delivered the message while omitting
  the delivery arrays. Provider failures expose only HTTP status and numeric
  Cloudflare error code, never the response body.
- Verification and rollout: Unit coverage must prove the exact endpoint,
  bearer header, bounded payload, configuration validation, success-state
  interpretation, and provider-body redaction using a local mock server.
  Production acceptance additionally requires a healthy sending domain,
  scoped-token installation, controlled backend restart, Cloudflare activity
  evidence, real inbox receipt, code login to `/me`, and unchanged Whop
  entitlement enforcement.
  Exact runtime `482c34d5` passed targeted Whop/email tests, immutable package
  hashing, zero-active-chat cutover, Cloudflare sender assembly, authoritative
  PostgreSQL/R2 health, and public route/auth/security checks. Current-secret
  signed no-side-effect probes passed locally and publicly; missing and
  body-tampered signatures returned `401`. A real non-owner buyer completing
  purchase through same-challenge inbox login remains the final business
  acceptance item.

## D-2026-07-30-01 Persist One Native Codex Thread Per Hone Logical Session

- Status: Accepted and locally verified with Codex CLI `0.146.0` and `@agentclientprotocol/codex-acp 1.1.7`.
- Superseded in part by: `D-2026-08-01-01` replaces `persistent_resume_v1`, first-turn transcript/system seeding, post-compact reseeding, and adapter `-c` assumptions. The persistent per-logical-session `session/new` / `session/resume` mapping and fail-closed resume behavior remain active.
- Supersedes in part: the Codex fresh-session/local-transcript portion of `D-2026-03-17-01` and the behavior introduced by `be5d7414`. OpenCode's fresh-session contract is unchanged.
- User-facing decision: One deterministic Hone logical session maps to one persistent native Codex session, so Codex page/history inspection shows the complete multi-turn conversation instead of one rollout per Hone message. This is per actor/session identity; no native thread is shared globally across users, direct chats, or channel scopes.
- Protocol decision: The first turn calls ACP `session/new` and stores both the returned native ID and `codex_acp_session_mode=persistent_resume_v1`. Later turns start a new adapter process but call `session/resume` for that exact ID before `session/prompt`. Hone does not call `session/load`: real adapter inspection and a cross-process probe showed that load replays historical `session/update` events, while resume returned no historical updates.
- Context ownership: On the first persistent entry only, Hone seeds the native thread from its durable normalized transcript and sends the complete static Hone system prompt. This preserves continuity when upgrading old one-turn metadata or switching into Codex from another runner. Ordinary trusted Codex Interactive turns omit restored transcript and static system prompt and send only one Beijing current-time value plus the normalized current user content, including actual attachment/image material. They do not repeat Hone session ID/history, receive-routing metadata, query-relevant skill hints, entity-tool-loop guidance, or final-answer contracts: native Codex owns retained history, tool/MCP lifecycle, and compaction, while stable Hone behavior already lives in the seeded system prompt. Explicit slash-skill expansion remains current user task content. When Codex ACP emits `_meta.contextCompaction=true` (with the legacy compact text retained as a fallback signal), Hone marks one pending reseed and sends the static system prompt exactly once on the next successful prompt. Native Codex history and automatic compaction remain authoritative. AgentSession does not locally compact and replay a native Codex context-overflow turn. OpenCode, non-Interactive scheduled/heartbeat work, and actor-safe strict fallback keep their existing Hone-assembled runtime context.
- Failure and migration boundary: Pre-marker `codex_acp_session_id` values are treated as legacy one-turn rollouts and are not resumed. If a marked persistent session cannot be resumed, the turn fails explicitly instead of silently creating another native thread. Operators must repair/reset that mapping deliberately.
- Configuration boundary: Model and reasoning effort remain Codex process `-c` settings established before ACP session creation or resume. No per-session `session/set_model` call is required.
- Verification: A real two-turn Discord-path probe kept Hone session `Actor_discord__direct__acp_5fpersistent_5fdiscord_5f20260730` and native Codex ID `019fb3c2-f2f7-7140-8140-7520409d79be` across both turns. ACP evidence contained one `session/new`, one `session/resume`, and two `session/prompt` calls; neither prompt re-injected restored transcript. The single native rollout file contained two `user_message`, two `agent_message`, and two task completions, and turn two returned the exact first-turn sentinel `ONE-CODEX-SESSION-20260730`. Codex Desktop's own task index listed that same ID as one local task. A 2026-07-31 real CLI follow-up used native ID `019fb5e4-7796-7410-bfb8-e5ab2fad887c`: prompt one contained the system seed; ordinary resumes did not. The latest resume was 103 characters, had no generic `### User Input ###` wrapper, and contained only `【当前时间】` and `【本轮用户输入】`; restored transcript, Session context, answer/entity contracts, and related-skill hints were all absent. All three sentinels completed exactly, `cargo test -p hone-channels --lib` passed 708 tests with one host-dependent OCR test ignored, and `cargo check -p hone-channels --tests` passed.

## D-2026-08-01-01 Separate Conversation Ownership From ACP Stream Dialects

- Status: Accepted and locally verified with Codex CLI `0.146.0`, `@agentclientprotocol/codex-acp 1.1.7`, and OpenCode `1.18.11`.
- Superseded in part by: `D-2026-08-03-11` replaces the fingerprint-compatible native generation boundary and its `new -> resume -> new` fixture expectation. The current-turn-only role boundary, conversation strategies, adapter-specific stream dialects, and fail-closed resume rule remain active.
- Supersedes in part: the Codex seed/reseed and process-`-c` portions of `D-2026-07-30-01`; the old `persistent_resume_v1` generation is historical and is never resumed by the new path.
- Conversation architecture: Every runner declares one `AgentConversationStrategy`: native persistence, structured replay, or ephemeral compiled prompt. Shared execution constructs a matching `RunnerConversationInput` only after selecting the actual runner. Codex receives only native developer instructions plus the current user turn; Hone Cloud receives structured roles; CLI and fresh-session OpenCode adapters may compile the system/current/history fields they explicitly own. Runner behavior is no longer inferred from overlapping `manages_own_context` booleans.
- Capability architecture: Conversation ownership, ACP stream dialect, and native workspace skill projection are separate typed axes. Only a runner declaring `NativeSkillProjection::CodexWorkspace` receives Codex-native skill links and their MCP allowlist filtering; native persistence alone never opts another runner into Codex-specific behavior.
- Codex role boundary: Hone passes the complete instruction layer as Codex `developer_instructions` through the official adapter `CODEX_CONFIG` environment, together with model/effort/safety settings. Every `session/prompt` contains only the canonical current turn, on `session/new`, `session/resume`, and after native compaction. Hone never sends system text, local user/assistant history, tool calls/results, or compact summaries through that user method, and compact events never set a reseed flag.
- Native generation boundary: Hone persists `codex_acp_session_mode=native_turn_v2` plus a SHA-256 instruction fingerprint. Only an exact mode/fingerprint match may resume. Legacy v1 or instruction-mismatched metadata creates a new native generation deliberately; an exact compatible resume still fails closed rather than silently forking.
- Stream architecture: ACP transport method names do not imply identical output. Each adapter declares a versioned stream dialect. The codex-acp `1.1.7` and OpenCode `1.18.11` paths retain every safely mappable answer, reasoning, tool, progress, usage, and terminal detail that the observed adapter exposes; channel output is not required to be byte-identical across adapters.
- Verification: An executable fake codex-acp boundary observed `session/new -> session/resume -> session/new` across same-instruction resume and changed-instruction rotation, emitted a compact notification between turns, and captured three exact current-turn-only prompts with no reseed metadata. A real two-turn `hone-cli probe` resumed native Codex ID `019fbe23-f728-7a72-a2fb-6ed9260d5e31`; rollout inspection found exactly two Hone current-turn user payloads, no cross-turn marker or system/history/tool replay, and both exact sentinel replies. A real OpenCode `1.18.11` ACP exchange returned `OPENCODE_ACP_OK`; its recorded fixture covers object-shaped thought/message chunks and `usage_update` detail (`used`, `size`, `cost`, `currency`).

## D-2026-07-31-01 Keep Conversational Notification Controls Deterministic And Domain-Owned

- Status: Accepted and locally verified.
- User-facing decision: A user may ask the Agent to adjust actor-scoped notification times and numeric thresholds: IANA timezone, named digest slots and their macro floor, quiet hours and exemptions, generic/up/down price thresholds, and the large-position weight boundary. Each nullable override can be restored independently to the system default. Prompt text, model selection, classifier policy, and investment-mainline text remain outside this ordinary conversational tool.
- Domain decision: `hone-event-engine::prefs` owns a typed `NotificationDeliveryPatch` with `Keep` / `Inherit` / `Set` states and one whole-preference validator. Patches apply to a clone and replace the live preference only after all ranges, time formats, slot identities, quiet-hour overlaps, and kind tags pass. Agent and HTTP paths consume this same contract instead of duplicating validation branches.
- Compatibility: Legacy `set_digest_slots` calls with an array of `HH:MM` strings still work. Structured calls preserve stable `id`, user-visible `label`, and optional `floor_macro`. Digest `null`, `[]`, and a non-empty list retain distinct inherit, disable, and custom semantics.
- Tool contract: The generic Tool trait supports a complete overridable input schema. `notification_prefs` publishes the actual string/number/boolean/array/object/null union accepted by its action/value protocol, so strict Agent runtimes can call structured settings without relying on schema violations. Single-field actions remain available, while `update_delivery_controls` submits a multi-field time/numeric transition as one atomic patch so old quiet/slot values cannot deadlock an otherwise valid final state.
- API and presentation: The existing actor-scoped REST full-write endpoint remains compatible but delegates validation to the domain layer. Notification overview output includes generic, directional, and large-position thresholds; the frontend API model carries those values even though this change does not add prompt or model controls.
- Verification: Full `hone-core` and `hone-event-engine` library suites, full `hone-tools` library tests, focused notification-prefs Web API tests, Web typecheck, and all 309 Web tests pass. The full Web API suite has one unrelated pre-existing attachment-prompt assertion failure in `public_chat_user_input_uses_shared_attachment_context`; the notification preference tests pass.

## D-2026-07-31-02 Use Codex-Native Discovery For Hone Skills

- Status: Accepted and locally verified with Codex CLI `0.146.0`.
- Scope: Trusted persistent Codex ACP conversations only. OpenCode, scheduled/transient work, and strict actor-bound runners retain their existing skill-loading contract.
- Decision: Before Codex ACP starts in an actor workspace, resolve the enabled Hone system/custom skills through the shared `SkillRuntime` registry and create one symlink per skill under `<actor sandbox>/.agents/skills/`. Codex sees the native metadata list and reads the selected `SKILL.md` on demand. The symlink target stays the source skill directory so bundled scripts/references remain available and source edits are immediately visible.
- Ownership boundary: Hone reserves only `hone__*` names for managed links, removes stale managed symlinks on later turns, and preserves all actor-owned files/directories/links. The actor workspace remains the runner cwd; it is not redirected to the repository.
- MCP boundary: Native Codex does not receive Hone's `SkillTool` loading prompt, query-relevant skill hints, or the `discover_skills` / `load_skill` / `skill_tool` MCP schemas. MCP remains available for live Hone tools such as market data, portfolio, scheduling, and search. Non-Codex/legacy runners continue to use the existing bridge.
- Verification: A real `hone-cli probe` produced 16 managed skill symlinks. Codex natively read `skills/market_analysis/SKILL.md` and returned its exact fixed phrase `原因本轮未完全核验`; the ACP tool trace contained only native file/command operations. The `session/new` MCP allowlist omitted all three skill-loading tools. A forced Codex `skills/list` returned all 16 Hone skills with `errors=[]`.

## D-2026-07-31-03 Keep Public-User Administration Separate And Database-Authoritative

- Status: Accepted and deployed in exact production runtime `5eacfe98`.
- Context: The remote public user experience is one responsive Web application shared by browser, macOS WebView, and iOS WKWebView. It already uses the domestic Web invite list as its login admission source, but the administrator console token and channel administrator lists are different trust domains and cannot authorize a public user.
- Identity decision: Cloud-mode public-user administrator authority is the explicit PostgreSQL `cloud_web_invite_users.is_admin` column. It is not embedded in membership JSON and is not inferred from the phone number or frontend state. `/api/public/auth/me` projects the flag for display only; each public-admin request authenticates the cookie session and rechecks PostgreSQL.
- Mutation decision: `cloud_web_admin_actions` is the audit and daily-count ledger. Public whitelist creation locks the administrator row, locks the normalized target phone scope, checks duplicates and the Beijing-date count, inserts the user, and appends the audit row in one transaction. Only successful creates count, and the hard ceiling is five per administrator per Beijing natural day. Disable is also transactional, clears active sessions, appends an audit row, and rejects self/administrator targets.
- API decision: The public administrator surface exposes only domestic phone, user id, timestamps, enabled state, and whether the current row may be disabled. Invite codes, API keys, password state, payment-provider details, and ordinary conversation quota are excluded. Mutating routes require a non-simple custom action header in addition to SameSite cookie and same-origin CORS boundaries.
- Read-availability decision: The management list reads a database-level minimal projection rather than deserializing full authentication records. Its daily-create counter is an ancillary control: if that read fails, creation is conservatively shown as exhausted while the list remains available. PostgreSQL Beijing-date parameters originate as Rust strings and must therefore bind as text before an explicit date cast; count and audit paths share this rule.
- Client decision: `packages/app` remains the only feature implementation. `/me` conditionally mounts one responsive management panel from the server-projected role; the native Apple shells inherit it without separate business logic or storage.
- Operational decision: `hone-cli cloud web-admin --phone <phone> --action grant|revoke` is dry-run by default and masks the phone in output. `--apply` performs a uniqueness-checked write and read-after-write verification. Direct ad hoc JSON editing is not the normal role-management workflow.

## D-2026-08-02-01 Select ACP Dialects From The Live Initialize Boundary

- Status: Accepted and locally verified with Codex CLI `0.146.0`, codex-acp `1.1.7`, and OpenCode `1.18.11`.
- Supersedes in part: the static runner-declared dialect portion of `D-2026-08-01-01`; conversation ownership and native skill projection remain unchanged.
- Version truth: Adapter kind is a static runner capability, but adapter identity/version evidence is not. The profile selector consumes and validates the matching `agentInfo.name/version` from the real connection's initialize result before any session or prompt. The Codex ACP `1.1.7` capture reports the exact package identity `@agentclientprotocol/codex-acp`, while OpenCode `1.18.11` reports `opencode`; unobserved shorthand aliases do not widen that contract. A second `--version` subprocess cannot select the stream dialect because it may resolve a different installation or race an update. Codex CLI remains a companion matrix axis and is probed from the exact executable used by the adapter.
- Compatibility policy: An exact capture is `validated`. A newer minor/patch within the same semantic-version major uses the nearest validated dialect with `compatible_newer` and a warning. Mismatched/missing identity, older versions, missing/unparseable versions, and unknown majors fail closed. The Codex CLI companion applies the same exact/same-major/unknown-major policy around `0.146.0`; a same-major fallback remains visibly unvalidated until a real captured exchange and fixture introduce a new baseline.
- Evidence policy: External fixtures under `tests/fixtures/acp/` carry adapter, exact version, and capture date. They preserve real wire variance—Codex execute/raw-output shapes versus OpenCode thought/split-message/usage shapes—without requiring cross-adapter bytes to match or copying private implementation output into an assertion.
- Projection policy: Typed reasoning never becomes final answer bytes. Full direct/admin surfaces may show sanitized reasoning progress; compact groups get a generic analysis signal; standard OpenAI output and unsuitable live-update channels omit it. Every visible fragment passes the shared path/internal-marker sanitizer first.
- Observability: A successful initialize atomically writes a bounded runtime profile. `/api/meta` reports the Web binary Git SHA/timestamp/profile, closed build-source kind, binary hash, and observed adapter profiles, including detected version, baseline/dialect, compatibility status, detection time, companion versions, and runner build SHA. Startup logs emit the same bounded build provenance; dialect-selection logs add the real adapter version/status and Codex CLI companion version/status. Empty profiles mean unobserved, and metadata/logs exclude paths, prompts, raw external fields, payloads, credentials, and user data.
- Verification: Unit contracts cover matching/mismatched identity, exact/newer/old/unknown-major/missing versions, bounded invalid diagnostics, Codex CLI unknown-major rejection, and sanitized persistence. Version-labelled Codex/OpenCode fixtures pass. Real Codex initialize/session-new passed; real OpenCode `1.18.11` completed initialize, `session/set_model`, a read-only Hone MCP call, streamed thought/answer/usage, and `end_turn` using an explicit free probe model. The configured default OpenAI OAuth separately returned `401` and is not misreported as production-provider success.

## D-2026-08-02-02 Deploy Direct Source Runtimes As Immutable Revision Units

- Status: Accepted and locally verified with exact implementation revision `ee9da19a9ac3d30e5df52c32dff7c40a387948dd`; GCE rollout is paused by user direction.
- Scope: Long-running macOS source Web plus the currently managed Discord listener. Foreground development startup and Vite processes remain separate workflows.
- Artifact decision: Build Web, Discord, and MCP with compile-time Git SHA/timestamp and the closed provenance kind `direct_source_runtime`; copy them into `data/releases/source/<sha>/`; hash every binary; and never replace an existing revision directory unless its manifest, source kind, and hashes already match. The running executable path, `/api/meta.build.git_sha`, and `/api/meta.build.source` must identify the requested revision and direct-source workflow.
- State decision: Preflight clean/expected/pushed revision, reject an unrecognized `8077` listener, drain real active chats, and identify either the managed Web/Discord jobs or the legacy `com.honeclaw.source.runtime` supervisor and its child PIDs. Remove the prior topology; if launchd reparents captured legacy children to PID 1, verify each PID still resolves to the captured executable, send TERM, and use bounded exact-PID KILL escalation only when it ignores the grace period. Wait exact supervisor/child PIDs and process locks, atomically install the revision-bound LaunchAgent plists, and bootstrap those exact files for both the canary and future login. The persisted runtime `PATH` is a stable explicit allowlist, never the invoking Codex turn's temporary `.codex/tmp` or `.cache/codex-runtimes` path; the selected runner executables must pass `--version` on that path before shutdown. Verify Web readiness/provenance and fresh Discord login, recoverably disable the legacy plist, then commit the `current` symlink. A launchd job's registered state is separate from child PID liveness.
- Failure decision: One exit trap owns rollback after shutdown begins. It removes every partially started managed job—even a loaded job whose child already crashed—waits locks, restores prior managed plists or restores and bootstraps the legacy supervisor plist, and verifies every service that was running before deployment. Partial success is never committed as current.
- Test boundary: The CI regression fakes only launchd/process/lock/HTTP/filesystem uncertainty. It proves clean/unpushed and unknown-owner refusal, direct-job and legacy-supervisor success, exact-plist bootstrap, persistent stable runner `PATH`, refusal of a disappeared turn-local Codex path, immutable build-source manifest, external `/api/meta` Git-SHA/source acceptance, exact parent/child PID observation, crashed-but-loaded Web/Discord jobs, readiness timeout, legacy bootstrap, and complete rollback without live ports, accounts, or provider credentials. It does not assert private shell function structure.

## D-2026-08-02-03 Keep Public Usage Analytics Inside The Web Administrator Boundary

- Status: Accepted and production verified at `c4c217236fae8bbe571f259cd46b6b4768178bcf`.
- Extends: `D-2026-07-31-03`; the database-authoritative administrator identity, cookie-session authentication, and per-request role recheck remain unchanged.
- Product decision: `/me` adds one responsive HONE usage report above whitelist management. It covers the latest 14 Beijing natural days and groups each active row by channel, actor user, and date. The table shows channel, question count and bounded question previews, scheduled execution count, successful delivery count, and requested-but-failed delivery count. It does not materialize zero-activity rows for every member. Two compact native-SVG charts show the same 14-day axis for distinct daily channel-scoped question users and daily question totals, explicitly filling missing dates with zero. Usage and whitelist panels are independently collapsible; the usage data remains one bounded, internally scrollable table with a sticky header on desktop and mobile.
- Summary decision: The visible headline is derived from the same server-returned rows as the selected table. The date selector materializes every date in `period_start..=period_end`, not only dates with rows, so zero-activity dates remain selectable and stable across refresh. “最近 14 天” reports unique question users and totals for the full window, then compares the latest seven days with the prior seven; a single date reports that date and compares it with the same Beijing date seven days earlier. If that prior date falls outside the returned window, the UI reports that comparison data is unavailable. A named decline user is the largest question-count decrease inside the comparison windows. Users who only received automated pushes do not count as usage users.
- Privacy and authority decision: Cross-user question text is sensitive administrator data. The report authenticates the Web cookie and re-reads the server role on every request. It accepts only concrete actors from supported `web`, `feishu`, `telegram`, `discord`, and `imessage` sessions; a group scope without an actor user id and unsupported channels fail closed. Identity is `(channel, user_id)`, because no authoritative cross-channel account binding exists. Web phone labels are masked, non-Web labels expose only the channel and a bounded identifier suffix, question previews are bounded, scheduler/heartbeat metadata, legacy `[定时任务触发]` envelopes, and any `user_id` whose trimmed case-insensitive prefix is `codex` are excluded; credentials, provider records, payment state, quota internals, and assistant answers remain absent.
- Data decision: Session history remains the truth source for user questions, and cron execution history remains the truth source for scheduled execution/delivery outcomes across supported channels. Trend charts derive from the complete returned 14-day row set: daily users are distinct `(channel, user_id)` actors with `question_count > 0`, daily questions are the sum of `question_count`, and push-only rows contribute to neither metric. Aggregation is read-only and generated on page load or explicit refresh; no analytics shadow table, event duplication, background poller, third-party chart dependency, or new client-side authority is introduced.
- Verification: Rust regressions cover UTC-to-Beijing date conversion, message automation exclusion, case-insensitive `codex*` actor exclusion, supported-channel inclusion, same external ids in separate channel namespaces, concrete group actors, multi-signal delivery counts, same-day comparison, and largest weekly decline selection. Web regressions cover API no-cache behavior, complete 14-day date options, zero-date refresh stability, channel-aware actor deduplication, selected-period summaries, 14-day trend zero-fill, collapse/table/chart contracts, and administrator-only mounting. Full Web typecheck, 344 Web tests, Public production build, 165 Web API tests with two credentialed live smokes ignored, and focused public-admin 8/8 passed. Authenticated production Chrome showed 334 rows across Feishu, Web, and Discord, 65 channel-scoped question actors, 401 questions, and 1,874 successful pushes for the then-current 14-day window; `codex*` actors were absent, August 1/2 summaries tracked the selector, the whitelist remained present, and the browser console had no warnings/errors.

## D-2026-08-02-04 Separate Price Candidate Bands From Actor Notification Ladders

- Status: Accepted and locally verified.
- Extends: `D-2026-04-24-01` keeps directional `price_band:*` event ids and `D-2026-07-31-01` keeps the deterministic actor notification patch contract. This decision replaces the assumption that an actor price override only lowers severity and that the system repeat step alone describes every user's cadence.
- Domain decision: `hone-event-engine::prefs` owns `PriceAlertPolicyDefaults` and `EffectivePriceAlertPolicy`. Canonical thresholds provide the system candidate first band, candidate step, default repeat step, non-large-position direct floor, large-position boundary, and close-direct flag. Actor common/directional first thresholds and the actor repeat-step override resolve against those defaults with explicit provenance. No runner, tool, Web route, or frontend independently recreates the policy.
- Routing decision: Price events are the only kind whose actor policy may both promote and demote severity. A user threshold below the system default can still promote a qualifying event subject to the system floor and existing large-position exception; a threshold above the system default demotes lower system High candidates to digest. `immediate_kinds=price_alert` cannot bypass this threshold. After the first qualifying intraday band, the next immediate push for the same actor/symbol/direction/local day must advance by at least the actor's effective repeat step; same/lower-band oscillation and insufficient advances enter digest. Up and down remain independent lanes.
- Candidate-grid decision: `PricePoller` continues to produce one shared bounded band grid from `price_alert_high_pct + N * price_realert_step_pct`; actor cadence is a router concern and does not multiply provider calls or global events. When an actor threshold or repeat target falls between candidate bands, the first candidate at or above the target is the observable result. The effective-policy response includes the candidate grid and concrete examples so the product never promises a threshold precision the source path cannot emit.
- Mutation and explanation decision: `price_realert_step_pct_override` is a nullable actor preference with `Keep` / `Inherit` / `Set` patch semantics and the same `(0, 50]` validation as price thresholds. Natural-language changes to the first threshold plus repeat step use one `update_delivery_controls` write. `notification_prefs.get_overview`, `/api/admin/schedule`, and the Web schedule page expose the structured effective policy and server-derived example ladders, plus the system event-engine switch, global disabled kinds, shared per-category daily High cap, and the fact that intraday price bands bypass the ordinary same-symbol cooldown; raw overrides remain present for editing but are not presented as the final behavior.
- Verification: The actor contract sequence proves that with an 8% first threshold and 4-point repeat step, ±6% is not immediate, ±8% is immediate, ±10% and a later regression to ±10% are not immediate, and ±12% / ±16% are immediate in independent directions. Domain tests cover inheritance provenance, non-grid thresholds, the system-floor/large-position exception, actor-final severity filtering, the shared daily cap, and the ordinary-cooldown exemption. Tool and schedule tests prove the same 8/12/16 policy plus system/global execution gates in structured JSON and every channel renderer; Web typecheck and tests cover the new preference and effective-policy models. The offline event-engine classifier baseline remains unchanged because this decision has no LLM classification dependency.

## D-2026-08-03-01 Project Confirmed Proactive Deliveries Into The Next Interactive Turn

- Status: Accepted and locally verified.
- Extends: `D-2026-08-01-01` conversation ownership and `D-2026-08-02-01` versioned ACP dialects. It does not restore Codex seed/reseed, local transcript replay, or adapter-neutral stream assumptions.
- Delivery boundary: Audit is not control flow. A proactive message enters the context journal only through explicit `EventStore::log_confirmed_delivery` after the channel-specific ACK or durable Web boundary. Ordinary audit rows—including rows labelled `sent`—cannot opt in implicitly. Discord and Telegram preserve only their acknowledged segment prefix; Feishu waits for send success; Web counts its durable push/history and iMessage waits for successful HTTP delivery. Failed, queued, dry-run, empty, and generated-but-unsent output stays out.
- State decision: Keep append-only `delivery_log` for operator truth and a separate `delivered_push_context` state table for consumption. The journal is keyed by actor and source id, ordered by millisecond delivery time, recoverably leased per Interactive turn, bounded by count and body characters, and not backfilled from historical audit. A five-second SQLite busy timeout plus an immediate claim transaction supports the event-engine/channel runtime connections that share the canonical `events.sqlite3`. A future deployment that separates these runtimes onto nodes without shared storage must implement the same interface on the authoritative cloud store rather than falling back to an in-memory queue.
- Turn decision: Capture the Interactive ingress cutoff before waiting on the session lock, so a push delivered after the user sent U1 cannot leak into U1. Quota rejection, `/compact`, scheduled, and heartbeat turns do not claim. Internal retry and overflow recovery reuse one typed batch. Agent success consumes it; failure releases it; an expired lease makes process-crash recovery possible. This is recoverable at-least-once delivery to Agent execution, not a false end-to-end exactly-once claim across external-process crashes.
- Projection decision: `RunnerConversationInput` owns projection. Native persistent runners receive a clearly labelled, instruction-resistant delivered-fact block immediately before the current `【本轮用户输入】`; the static developer layer, local transcript, tools, tool results, and compact summary remain absent. Structured replay and fresh-session compiled runners receive the same fact as a metadata-tagged assistant/context record while their current user turn stays byte-identical. If the same native session already generated and retained a scheduled output, the journal advances without reinjecting that output; replay runners still receive it because their owned transcript filters automation.
- Compatibility decision: The Codex ACP `1.1.7` executable JSON-RPC boundary proves the resulting `session/prompt` contains only current time, optional delivered facts, and current user input even after a compaction signal. The OpenCode `1.18.11` prompt contract keeps delivered facts in assistant/context before `### User Input ###` and never splices them into the user section. These version labels preserve observed external uncertainty without requiring Codex and OpenCode to expose identical detail.
- Verification: Store regressions cover explicit confirmation versus audit-only `sent`, actor/scope isolation, ordering, source retry dedupe, cutoff races, stable re-entry, success/failed consumption, native observation, old-database migration, two independent SQLite connections, and body budgets. Session/runner regressions cover P1/P2 before U1, no repeat in U2, persisted user bytes, failure release, structured assistant roles, Codex ACP `1.1.7`, and OpenCode `1.18.11`. Full event-engine and channel library suites plus all affected channel/Web targets pass before repository-wide gates.

## D-2026-08-03-02 Reject Malformed Read-only Calls Individually And Require Listing Evidence

- Status: Accepted and locally verified; exact production replacement rollout is in progress.
- Refines: `D-2026-07-19-08` and `D-2026-07-26-06` retain one Interactive Agent and no general post-answer semantic review. `D-2026-07-22-01` retains whole-batch zero execution for unknown, unregistered, write-capable, persistent, or effect-uncertain calls, but its all-or-nothing treatment of malformed registered known-read-only siblings is replaced.
- Context: After exact release `116dc54b` added current SNDK listing markers and one production canary passed, a real public Web request still answered that SNDK was delisted and redirected to WDC. An audit-enabled non-admin Web canary reproduced the exact branch: the model emitted a valid SNDK identity search, a malformed symbol-dependent `earnings_outlook`, and an independent Web search in one batch. One malformed read-only argument object cancelled every valid sibling; the tools-disabled continuation then published stale 2016 acquisition memory without current inactivity evidence.
- Execution decision: When every proposed call is registered and classified known read-only, validate each call independently. A malformed call returns a structured `invalid_read_only_tool_shape` / `rejected_before_execution` tool result and never reaches observer, registry, provider, or finance ledger; valid read-only siblings execute normally. Any unknown, unregistered, persistent, write-capable, or effect-uncertain sibling still keeps the complete batch at zero execution. This is failure isolation, not permission to reinterpret a malformed call or guess its missing security target.
- Publication decision: For an explicit current-turn security symbol, affirmative “delisted/not listed” output requires current same-symbol `inactive_listing` evidence. Current same-symbol `active_listing`, or the conservative combination of a positive quote and `isActivelyTrading=true` profile with a nonempty exchange, contradicts such a denial. Absence, partial coverage, historical acquisition memory, and old parent-company relationships do not prove inactivity. The same Agent may receive one hidden correction with current evidence; if it repeats the denial or no inactivity evidence exists, the runtime publishes only a deterministic evidence-gap statement and never redirects to the historical parent. This narrow fact boundary does not score general prose, synthesize an investment answer, or add a second terminal role.
- Verification and rollout: Agent regressions must reproduce the exact SNDK valid-search/malformed-outlook/Web batch, prove only valid siblings execute and the rejected call stays auditable, provide active listing evidence, force a stale delisting draft, and observe a corrected same-Agent final. Separate tests cover explicit inactive evidence, quote/profile composition, lowercase symbols adjacent to CJK, repeated-denial fallback, and unchanged whole-batch blocking for unknown/write-capable calls. Production acceptance requires repeated fresh non-admin Web strict-runner canaries after the exact build, not one lucky tool sequence, plus zero active chats, cloud authority, exact metadata, public auth boundaries, and quota 100 verification.

## D-2026-08-03-03 Bound Local Build Storage Without Sharing Writable Targets

- Status: Accepted and locally verified; no runtime deployment was performed.
- Extends: `D-2026-08-02-02`; immutable revision identity, external provenance checks, and complete pre-commit rollback remain unchanged.
- Context: The observed near-50 GiB footprint was not one clean build. It was dominated by three independent worktree targets: the canonical checkout retained about 36 GiB (`deps` about 22 GiB and `incremental` about 12 GiB), another clean Codex worktree retained about 9.9 GiB, and the active worktree retained about 3.8 GiB. Frontend bundles were only tens of MiB. Default full dev debuginfo, test/deploy incremental state, repeated revision artifacts, and seven immutable runtime packages amplified each other.
- Profile decision: The built-in dev and test profiles use `debug=1` and `incremental=false`. Revision-bound source deployment uses a dedicated root `source-runtime` profile with the same settings, writing to that worktree's `target/source-runtime/`; the release manifest records `cargo_profile=source-runtime`. This keeps useful line-level panic/backtrace evidence without preserving full local-variable debug graphs or incremental object graphs. Binaries are not stripped.
